Banks 
Q: What are the implications of the recent rise in rates across the curve for banks? 
A: Higher rates impact banks through three primary channels: 1) net interest income (NII), 2) credit quality, 
and 3) mark-to-market changes in bank securities’ fair value.  
Net interest income (NII): We expect higher rates to have an overall positive impact on US banks’ NII given 
their asset-sensitive balance sheets, where asset yields reprice faster than liabilities. Higher long rates 
accelerate fixed-rate asset repricing as maturing loans and securities roll over into higher coupon 
assets. While higher short-term interest rates put upward pressure on funding costs, for most banks 
this is more than offset by the repricing of floating-rate loans and securities, as well as higher yields on 
cash held at central banks. Higher interest rates can also weigh on loan growth given that elevated 
borrowing costs adversely impact demand. However, the historical relationship between changes in rates and loan growth is 
relatively weak, with consumer spending and corporate confidence generally the larger drivers of loan demand. 
Credit quality: Higher interest rates can increase delinquency and default risks, particularly in commercial real estate (CRE), where 
maturing debt is refinanced into higher coupon debt. However, a significant deterioration in unemployment or corporate bankruptcy 
rates has historically driven meaningful increases in nonperforming loans (NPLs), neither of which has occurred so far in this cycle. 
Accordingly, we do not expect the recent increase in rates to materially impact credit quality unless it is accompanied by broader 
economic deterioration. 
Changes in securities values: Higher rates will also result in negative marks on banks’ securities portfolios, decreasing their capital 
positions. However, we expect the impact of this shift to be manageable this quarter. We estimate that marks for available-for-sale 
(AFS) portfolios will decline by ~0.6%, resulting in a ~13bps reduction to CET1 ratios and ~8bps hit to tangible common equity-to-
tangible assets (TCE/TA). Most banks should retain excess capital even after accounting for these losses. That said, the risk of 
further rate increases, alongside continued growth in bank balance sheets, could lead some banks to moderate buybacks in coming 
quarters to ensure that they maintain capital cushions. Higher rates also impact held-to-maturity (HTM) securities portfolios, which 
are larger than AFS portfolios. However, these losses do not impact a bank’s capital position and pose limited risk to the banking 
system unless material deposit outflows force banks to realize losses in their HTM book. So far, deposit flows into the banking 
industry have remained positive (+6% YoY and 1% QoQ over the last 3 months) and HTM losses remain meaningfully below 2023 
levels, reflecting both shorter securities duration across banks (the duration has fallen from 4.8 years to 3.7 years since 2023) and 
smaller overall HTM portfolios. 
Q: Does the above calculus shift at all for smaller/regional banks? 
A: Regional banks are more sensitive to interest rate movements than large-cap banks because they generally have less diversified 
revenue streams and therefore rely more heavily on spread income. By contrast, large banks typically generate a greater share of 
their revenues from capital markets and wealth management businesses, reducing their rate sensitivity. 
Q: Where do you see the most attractive investment opportunities if rates remain elevated?  
A: In a higher rate environment, we prefer banks with high-quality deposit bases as those deposits are largely operational and not 
yield-seeking and therefore face limited upward pressure from higher rates. We also favor banks positioned to benefit from higher 
long-term rates as fixed-rate assets reprice.

### Page 21

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 21 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Private markets 
Q: What are the implications of the recent rise in rates across the curve for private markets? 
A: The rise in rates is generally negative for private markets, though the implications vary by asset class 
and, ultimately, the level of rates. At a high level, higher rates across the curve typically result in (1) lower 
levels of monetization and deployment activity in private equity, real estate, and the direct lending part of 
private credit (loans to sponsor-backed portfolio companies) and (2) potential headwinds to portfolio 
valuations, particularly in growth-oriented private equity assets and real estate.  
Most private equity assets are funded with floating-rate loans, so the 
short-end of the curve tends to have a larger impact on financial 
sponsors’ deployment and realization activity. When it comes to 
valuations, we believe much of this risk is hedged out, reducing the 
ultimate impact on portfolio companies’ cash flows and investment 
performance, but higher-growth companies where most of the value 
is predicated on terminal value could be more negatively impacted. 
In private real estate, the long end of the curve tends to matter more 
for both valuation and activity rates. Direct lending could also see 
slower activity rates as it is directly linked to private equity capital 
deployment. And higher borrowing costs from floating rates could 
weigh on cash flows and credit performance, though the absolute risk 
of a ~100bp move would be manageable.  
Based on what we have seen so far across both the long and the 
short end of the curve (including market expectations for future rate 
hikes), we see the slowdown in private market activity as relatively 
temporary, with rate volatility, as opposed to the currently higher absolute level of rates, driving incremental uncertainty. 
Q: If the rise in rates continues, at what point would you become more concerned about the outlook for private markets? 
A: Rates rising +/- 100bps across the curve should not be a material headwind to private markets, and we expect activity to 
resume once the level of rates stabilizes. However, if rates rise significantly more, we expect more concerns around the valuation 
of longer-duration assets, such as real estate and growth-oriented private equity. A more prolonged hit to exit activity would also 
further delay the return of capital to investors (which has been running around two years behind typical timelines), leading to lower 
fundraising outlooks. 
Q: What do you believe investors may be too concerned about, or underappreciating, about how higher yields could 
impact private markets? 
A: Although higher rates are a net negative to private markets, the absolute level of rates matters. So far, the rise in yields of 
around +/-100bps has been relatively manageable, and activity rates are likely to resume once rates volatility stabilizes. Moreover, 
credit spreads remain tight by historical standards, and economic growth (a larger driver of value across private equity) remains on 
a strong footing. We think investors are likely overestimating the fundamental damage of higher rates on alternative managers, 
leading to stock price selloffs of >20% that represent a significant overshoot relative to the low-to-mid single-digit risks to 2027 
earnings that we forecast in our base case. We note that the industry has spent the better part of the last three years operating in 
a higher rate environment, during which time fundraising, deployment, and realization activity have all experienced periods of 
pressure but continued to grow on a multi-year basis.  
We think investors are also underappreciating the robust structural tailwinds that help offset macro headwinds, including (1) the 
earnings lever of the AI capex cycle across infrastructure, private credit, and syndication businesses; (2) the highly resilient 
fundraising trends; (3) the stabilization of retail alternative channels, where private credit redemption requests have peaked and are 
beginning to moderate alongside strong momentum in non-credit vehicles; (4) potentially stronger returns from newer vintages in 
private credit/direct lending amid higher base rates (since loans are floating rate); and (5) a potential acceleration in secondary 
market activity as LP liquidity is further pushed out by slower realizations. 
Q: Where do you see the most attractive investment opportunities if rates remain near current levels or continue to rise? 
A: If rates remain at current levels or move modestly higher, we would continue to see the most attractive opportunity set in 
infrastructure, private credit, and secondaries. In infrastructure and private credit, the secular investment cycle around AI-related 
power, energy, and digital infrastructure remains one of the most significant capital deployment opportunities in private markets 
today, supporting both deployment volumes and earnings growth for managers with relevant exposure. And in secondaries, 
depressed realizations and persistent pressure on distributed to paid-in capital (DPI) continue to drive a favorable supply-demand 
backdrop, creating opportunities to acquire assets at attractive discounts to net asset value.

### Page 22

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 22 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Christian Mueller-Glissmann and Andrea 
Ferrario argue that higher yields strengthen 
the strategic case for bond allocations 
Over the past five years, US 10-year bonds delivered the worst 
return in over a century—with real returns declining to levels 
seen only in the aftermath of WWI and WWII and in the 
1970s—as global bond yields have risen sharply toward their 
long-run average from unusually low levels during the post-
Global Financial Crisis (GFC) cycle (at 5.2%, the US 10-year 
yield is modestly above its 4.7% average over the past 250 
years). These higher starting yields should improve carry, lift 
medium- to long-term total returns, and provide a larger 
portfolio buffer against further yield increases. This argues for a 
larger strategic allocation to bonds than over the past five 
years, but we do not expect an immediate return to the 60/40 
portfolio, as strong earnings growth, sticky inflation, and capital 
competition still favor equities over bonds, at least for now. 
US bonds recently had one of the worst returns in a century 
5-year rolling returns of 10y Treasury bonds, %

Source: Haver Analytics, Robert Shiller, Goldman Sachs GIR. 
Finding the right balance 
The optimal equity/bond mix has varied materially over time, 
largely as a function of macro conditions. Since 1950, the 60/40 
portfolio has been close to optimal on average, but the optimal 
mix has shifted significantly in recent decades. From the GFC 
to the Covid-19 pandemic, the optimal equity/bond allocation 
was closer to 40/60. But over the past five years, it has shifted 
to 100% equities, as most investors would have been better 
off pairing equities with cash than holding a balanced portfolio. 
An improving tactical and strategic case for bonds… 
However, higher yields are shifting the calculus for bonds, but 
their attractiveness depends on the investment horizon. In the 
near term, monetary policy and energy prices should remain 
the key drivers of equities and bonds. Further yield increases—
particularly if driven by higher energy prices or more aggressive 
central bank tightening—would likely weigh more on equities 
than bonds, with increases above two standard deviations over 
three months historically triggering particularly large equity 
declines. Conversely, lower energy prices, continued core 
inflation normalization, and less hawkish central banks should 
help bonds stabilize into year-end and support equities.  
Over medium- to long-term horizons, higher starting yields 
should improve carry and buy-and-hold returns while also giving 
bonds better asymmetry as the potential gains from rate relief 
now exceed the likely losses from a further rise in yields. 
Accordingly, the case for higher strategic bond allocations is 
stronger now than in the past five years, and we see the 
optimal share of bonds in portfolios normalizing back toward 
the long-run optimal range of 35-55%.  
…but not quite a 60/40 balance yet 
However, we do not expect a return to the traditional 60/40 
portfolio anytime soon as strong earnings growth still favors 
equities over bonds in the near and medium term. Prospective 
global equity carry—the starting dividend yield plus subsequent 
change in earnings—has increased sharply this year, supported 
by the AI investment boom. Our equity strategists expect 
slower but still strong earnings growth next year, which should 
keep risk-adjusted equity returns attractive relative to bonds, 
even as relative returns moderate. That said, the case for 
higher bond allocations would strengthen on a 6-12m horizon if 
AI-driven earnings tailwinds fade or growth slows materially. 
For now, bonds’ diversification benefits are also likely to remain 
weaker than during the post-GFC period as equity/bond 
correlations should be less negative with less firmly anchored 
inflation. And even over a 10-year horizon, equities are likely to 
drive most of the variation across portfolio outcomes. 
Structural regimes point to different long-term return forecasts 
10y return scenarios (lhs, %) vs. optimal bond weights (rhs, %)

Q&A on US Treasury buybacks 
Q: What are Treasury buyback operations and how do they work? 
A: The Treasury conducts buyback operations generally once or twice a week by purchasing off-the-run Treasury securities 
(securities that are not the most recently issued) from primary dealers and other select market participants up to a pre-announced 
cap. While the Treasury is under no obligation to buy back any securities, it often purchases the maximum amount. The Treasury 
funds these purchases by issuing additional short-term debt, effectively removing longer-term debt from the market and replacing it 
with shorter-term debt.  
Q: Why does the Treasury conduct such operations?  
A: The purpose of buyback operations is to bolster market liquidity by establishing a “regular and predictable” opportunity for 
market participants to sell off-the-run securities. Bonds tend to become less liquid as they age, so by offering periodic opportunities 
for dealers to reduce inventories of these securities, the Treasury helps ensure sufficient liquidity and supports overall market 
functioning across the maturity spectrum.  
Q: What did the US Treasury recently announce regarding its buyback operations?  
A: On August 19, the Treasury announced that it would at least double the size of its buyback operations for longer-dated 
government debt (the 10- to 20-year and 20- to 30-year maturity buckets), from $2bn/operation to at least $4bn/operation. The 
announcement was notable given that the buyback schedule is typically set at each Quarterly Treasury Refunding—the Treasury’s 
quarterly announcement of its borrowing strategy and upcoming debt auction sizes—making a mid-quarter change to operation 
sizes unusual.  
Q: How meaningful is the announced increase in buybacks?  
A: While the expanded operation is meaningful from a long-end supply perspective—at the new upsized amount, long-end buybacks 
should offset at least 30% of gross 10-30 year issuance annually, and 45% if the Treasury uses the entire $6bn cap it has 
announced so far for each buyback operation—it is small relative to overall Treasury supply. The stock of US Treasury debt with at 
least 10 years to maturity stands at around $4tn, or around 15% of privately held debt outstanding. While the larger buybacks 
should keep that share roughly stable over the coming years following several decades of steady increases, they will likely have a 
much more marginal impact on the total debt outstanding as a share of GDP.  
The dislocation of the Treasury curve vs. a modeled fitted curve remains 
relatively contained, signaling little evidence that higher yields have 
been due to poor liquidity or an oversupply of long-end Treasuries 
US Treasury curve dislocations from model fitted values

Source: Goldman Sachs GIR. 
Q: Why did the Treasury increase the operation size? 
A: The Treasury cited liquidity support as the 
justification, pointing to the “significant volume of high-
quality offers it routinely receives in longer-dated 
buyback operations” as evidence of strong dealer 
demand to sell older securities. That said, we see little 
evidence that yields have risen due to poor liquidity or 
an oversupply of long-end Treasuries, with macro 
fundamentals the main drivers of the recent bond selloff 
(see pgs. 10-11). The more likely motivation for the 
increase in buybacks looks to be a desire to temper the 
rise in longer-term bond yields. That said, the Treasury’s 
purchase of over $4bn of long-dated bonds during each 
of the last three buyback operations has repeatedly 
fallen short of the $6bn cap—implying that it is not 
willing to purchase bonds regardless of price.     
Q: Has the increase in buybacks impacted the level of bond yields? 
A: The larger buybacks have had very little impact on the level of yields so far, for a few key reasons. First, as mentioned, the shift 
in supply is relatively small compared to the overall size of the Treasury market. Our typical rule of thumb for the impact of duration 
removal on the level of yields argues for a relatively modest impact. We estimate that the Fed’s 2011 Operation Twist program 
lowered 10-year yields by around 4bp per percent of GDP. Assuming a similar impact today, the buybacks would imply around a 1bp 
decline in 10-year yields levels. Second, as mentioned, the way in which the Treasury has conducted the operations suggests it 
remains a discerning—not indiscriminate—buyer. Third, and most importantly, removing long-end supply does little to address the 
macro fundamentals that have driven the selloff.  
Q: What else can the Treasury do if it wants to lower yields? 
A: The Treasury can potentially ease pressures on long-end bonds by reducing long-end auction sizes or doing away with 20-year 
issuance. Treasuries in other major economies, such as the UK and Japan, recently took similar actions, which had some impact—in 
Japan, it supported a reduction in long-end risk premium —but in both cases the efforts have failed to arrest the macro pressures 
that have taken long-end yields to cycle highs. Similarly, in the US, with the latest leg of the bond selloff having affected both long- 
and medium-term yields, any issuance adjustments at the long end may have only a limited impact.  
  Isabella Rosenberg, Senior US Rates Strategist    
  Email:   isabella.rosenberg@gs.com    Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC 
  Tel:    212-357-7628

Top of Mind Issue 152

Current Activity Indicator (CAI) 
GS CAIs measure the growth signal in a broad range of weekly and monthly indicators, offering an alternative to Gross 
Domestic Product (GDP). GDP is an imperfect guide to current activity: In most countries, it is only available quarterly and is 
released with a substantial delay, and its initial estimates are often heavily revised. GDP also ignores important measures of real 
activity, such as employment and the purchasing managers’ indexes (PMIs). All of these problems reduce the effectiveness of 
GDP for investment and policy decisions. Our CAIs aim to address GDP’s shortcomings and provide a timelier read on the pace 
of growth.  
For more, see our CAI page and Global Economics Comment: Technical Updates to Our Global CAIs.  
Dynamic Equilibrium Exchange Rates (DEER) 
The GSDEER framework establishes an equilibrium (or “fair”) value of the real exchange rate based on relative productivity and 
terms-of-trade differentials.  
For more, see our GSDEER page, Global Economics Paper No. 227: Finding Fair Value in EM FX, 26 January 2016, and Global 
Markets Analyst: A Look at Valuation Across G10 FX, 29 June 2017. 
Financial Conditions Index (FCI) 
GS FCIs gauge the “looseness” or “tightness” of financial conditions across the world’s major economies, incorporating 
variables that directly affect spending on domestically produced goods and services. FCIs can provide valuable information 
about the economic growth outlook and the direct and indirect effects of monetary policy on real economic activity.  
FCIs for the G10 economies are calculated as a weighted average of a policy rate, a long-term risk-free bond yield, a corporate 
credit spread, an equity price variable, and a trade-weighted exchange rate; the Euro area FCI also includes a sovereign credit 
spread. The weights mirror the effects of the financial variables on real GDP growth in our models over a one-year horizon. FCIs 
for emerging markets are calculated as a weighted average of a short-term interest rate, a long-term swap rate, a CDS spread, 
an equity price variable, a trade-weighted exchange rate, and—in economies with large foreign-currency-denominated debt 
stocks—a debt-weighted exchange rate index.  
For more, see our FCI page, Global Economics Analyst: Our New G10 Financial Conditions Indices, 20 April 2017, and Global 
Economics Analyst: Tracking EM Financial Conditions – Our New FCIs, 6 October 2017. 
Goldman Sachs Analyst Index (GSAI) 
The US GSAI is based on a monthly survey of GS equity analysts to obtain their assessments of business conditions in the 
industries they follow. The results provide timely “bottom-up” information about US economic activity to supplement and cross-
check our analysis of “top-down” data. Based on analysts’ responses, we create a diffusion index for economic activity 
comparable to the ISM’s indexes for activity in the manufacturing and nonmanufacturing sectors. 
Macro-Data Assessment Platform (MAP) 
GS MAP scores facilitate rapid interpretation of new data releases for economic indicators worldwide. MAP summarizes the 
importance of a specific data release (i.e., its historical correlation with GDP) and the degree of surprise relative to the 
consensus forecast. The sign on the degree of surprise characterizes underperformance with a negative number and 
outperformance with a positive number. Each of these two components is ranked on a scale from 0 to 5, with the MAP score 
being the product of the two, i.e., from -25 to +25. For example, a MAP score of +20 (5;+4) would indicate that the data has a 
very high correlation to GDP (5) and that it came out well above consensus expectations (+4), for a total MAP value of +20.  
Glossary of GS proprietary indices

## Document 418: Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Tracking Europe’s AI transition:  
A new STOXX 600 earnings-call 
framework

1 
Authors 
Michael Kirker 
Economist

November 11, 2026 
How quickly is AI moving from experimentation into everyday business, and 
where are companies reporting tangible gains? The answers matter for 
institutional investors and policymakers assessing productivity, operating 
efficiency and labour demand. Yet comparable evidence across Europe remains 
limited, making it difficult to track the pace and scale of the transition. 1 
We address this gap with a proprietary framework that turns what is said on 
STOXX 600 earnings call into three structured indicators: AI adoption 
intensity, reported productivity benefits, and the impact on workforces. 
Large language models analyse what management teams say about AI’s 
operational role, applying a common scoring framework across companies, 
sectors and countries. Our new approach provides a timely pan-European 
comparable tracker that captures not just whether AI is being mentioned, but its 
real-world impact as the adoption of the new technology unfolds. 
Key Takeaways: 
— Adoption is broadening, with signs of faster progress. AI-use scores rose 
gradually from a low base before strengthening over the past 12 months. The 
increase extends across sectors, although the pace and level of adoption 
remain uneven. 
— Workforce impacts remain limited in corporate disclosures. The 
framework does not show a broad negative workforce signal attributable to 
AI. The aggregate score remains close to the level of no impact on the labour 
force across all of our sample. However, this does not rule out AI displacing 
workers at a future date. 
— Geographic differences persist within the listed-company sample. Since 
2022, German companies in the STOXX 600 have generally recorded above-
average AI-use and reported productivity scores. Dutch and Spanish 
companies in the STOXX 600 have tended to score lower on AI use, although 
Spain has recently gone through a rapid catch up. 
— The technology J-Curve: The findings are consistent with an early stage of 
the AI transition. Companies report increasing adoption of the new 
technology, but transformational productivity gains and extensive workforce 
restructuring remain uncommon.

1 1 Our US team regularly publish on data from the USCB showing the impact of 
AI on businesses, and our German team have recently begun their own 
chartbook. In the UK, the Office for National Statistics publishes on the topic too.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

Methodology 
Sample period: Our sample begins in January 2022, providing a baseline before 
ChatGPT’s launch on 30 November 2022. AI in this framework covers both 
established and emerging technologies, so the launch of ChatGPT is a reference 
point rather than the start of all corporate AI adoption. 
Timing of observations: STOXX 600 companies report on different financial 
calendars and at quarterly or semi-annual intervals. Their earnings calls also mix 
discussion of completed activity with forward-looking guidance. 
We assign each transcript to the calendar quarter in which the earnings call takes 
place, regardless of the financial reporting period discussed. The resulting 
indicators therefore track when information about AI use and its operational 
impacts becomes public rather than when implementation or the reported effect 
occurred. This provides a common dating convention across the sample while 
retaining differences in reporting frequency and disclosure timing.

Figure 1: Coverage of the STOXX 600 earnings calls

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: Share of the STOXX 600 is based on the current company weight in the index. Data collection for the current quarter (Q4 2026) is incomplete 
as some companies are still to report

Constructing the STOXX 600 AI corpus: We use a specialised AI transcription 
engine to process earnings calls from STOXX 600 companies, restricting the 
analysis to calls for which we obtain complete transcripts of sufficient accuracy. 
In Q3 2026, the latest full quarter, the dataset covered more than 500 companies, 
representing approximately 90% of the index’s current market capitalisation 
weights (Figure 1). In the current quarter (Q4) earnings calls are still taking place. 
Nearly 250 companies with an equivalent weight to around c.60% of the STOXX 
index having already reported. 
Historical coverage typically ranges from 300 to 500 companies and represents 
more than 80% of the index’s current market-capitalisation weights. Coverage 
peaks twice a year because some European companies report semi-annually.

For examples of the AI’s scoring and justifications, see the tables presented in the 
Annex at the end of this report. 
Summary statistics 
Figure 2 shows the distribution of scores across all earnings-call transcripts in the 
historical sample, without weighting for company size or reporting frequency. 
These are transcript-level distributions rather than shares of companies, and they 
combine early and recent observations. They provide context for the quarterly 
indicators: 
• AI use score: Approximately 60% of earnings call transcripts do not 
discuss AI use. Among the minority that do discuss it, most fall into the 
“Active implementation” range (a score of 4-6 out of 10). 
• AI’s productivity impact: Nearly 70% of earnings calls do not mention a 
tangible AI-related productivity benefit. Among calls that do, scores are 
generally in the medium-impact band, indicating notable operational 
improvements. Transformational gains, scored at 8 or above out of 10, 
are rarely reported. 
• AI’s labour impact: Only around 10% of earnings calls discuss AI-related 
workforce adjustments. Reported changes typically receive scores of 1–
2 in magnitude on the −5 to +5 scale, with a slight tilt towards hiring and 
upskilling rather than redundancies.

Figure 2: Raw distribution of scores across the entire sample

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Note: Results are based on the raw number of transcripts, and are independent of firm size, and biased towards firms reporting more often.

Taken together, the distributions are consistent with an early adoption phase: 
companies discuss AI implementation more often than transformational 
productivity gains or extensive workforce restructuring. A technology J-curve 
offers one interpretation of this pattern, with investment and organisational 
adaptation preceding broader benefits.  
This J-curve pattern is strongly corroborated by external micro-data, such as the 
Banco de España’s business activity survey (EBAE) of Spanish firms, which 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

similarly indicates that AI has a more immediate impact on upfront investment 
and productivity than on headcount. 
AI use: A broad-based rise, with signs of faster recent adoption 
The new AI-use indicator points to a broadening transition across STOXX 600 
companies, with early signs that adoption has accelerated recently. Scores rose 
only gradually from 2022 and 2024, but the latest observations suggest a 
stronger pace across industries and country groups.  
The rise is broad-based rather than confined to one sector. Technology has led 
much of the sample, while financials show tentative recent outperformance and 
utilities have caught up from a low starting point. Among the large country groups, 
German companies have persistently recorded above-average scores.  
AI use across European industries 
To trace sectoral dispersion, we group companies by STOXX industry 
classification and calculate a weighted average of their scores for each calendar 
quarter, using their latest STOXX 600 index weights. 2  
We focus on four sectors spanning different business models: financials, the 
largest services sector; industrials, the largest physical-assets sector; technology, 
a likely early adopter; and utilities, an infrastructure-heavy sector where adoption 
may proceed more slowly.  
Figure 3 presents the AI use score for these four key industries alongside the 
range for all industry scores (represented by the shaded area). 
• The AI use score is generally low over time but is gradually rising from 
around 1 point in 2022 to around 3 in 2026. 
• AI-use scores are rising across industries, but differences between 
sectors are widening. The common direction therefore points to 
broadening adoption rather than uniform progress: sectors can 
participate in the transition while remaining at different levels of use. 
In terms of the industries of interest: 
• Technology’s early lead has narrowed. The sector’s AI-use score was 
near the top of the industry range through to 2024 but has recently 
plateaued as the broader STOXX 600 average caught up during 2025–
26.3 
• Financials and industrials broadly track the index average. Both sectors 
have remained close to the aggregate AI-use score over history, with 
financials generally a little below average, and industrials a little above. 
But financials show tentative signs of moving above average in recent 
quarters.

2 The industries are: (1) Basic Materials, (2), Consumer Discretionary, (3), Consumer Staples, (4) Energy, (5) 
Financials, (6) Health Care, (7) Industrials, (9) Real Estate, (10) Technology, (11) Telecommunications, (12) 
Utilities. 
3 Q4 2025 saw a large increase in the AI use score for the “Technology” industry, jumping up to around 6. This 
one-off spike was driven by ASML’s announcement of a significant strategic partnership with and investment 
in Mistral AI” to 'embed AI across our entire holistic portfolio in order to increase the performance and 
productivity of our systems and the yield of our customers' processes'. Because of ASML’s large market cap 
(around 49% of our Technology category), it distorts the weighted average significantly.

• Utilities have caught up from a low base. After recording lower AI-use 
scores than other sectors for much of the sample, utilities converged 
towards the STOXX 600 average during 2026. The result shows that the 
rise in reported adoption extends beyond the sectors most closely 
associated with AI.

Figure 3: AI use score (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The value for each industry is computed as a weighted average of all firms within the industry where the weights are derived from the current 
STOXX 600 share of each company. The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a cross-check, our findings align with the European Commission’s October 
Eurobarometer Flash survey of MSEs and Mid-Caps and the Eurostat annual ICT 
surveys, which show low overall intensity but a similar expansion in service- and 
tech-related sectors.  
AI use across countries 
Companies in the STOXX 600 tend to be large multi-nationals. That makes it 
difficult to pin down where physically the AI transformation is taking place within 
their organisation. Instead, our country comparison is done using two 
classifications of a company’s country: 
• Country of domicile: AI is a transformation technology, and this type of 
change is likely to be driven in organisation from the top down. We 
therefore consider a company’s country as that in which it is domiciled 
(which is likely to be where the headquarters is also). 
• Country of primary listing: While companies can generally list in other 
countries, there tends to be a home bias in their choice of exchange.  
Figure 4 presents the AI use intensity metric by both of these measures of country 
(the company’s domicile and primary exchange listing). Again, the shaded area 
shows the range across all classifications, with the largest countries/exchanges 
singled out. 
German-domiciled companies and the Xetra-listed (Frankfurt stock exchange) 
group record above-average AI-use scores in most quarters since 2022.  
Spain’s AI-use score has risen noticeably over recent quarters and now exceeds 
the aggregate average, reversing its earlier below-average position.  
11 November 2026
Focus Europe

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

AI-use scores rise across country groups, with indications of faster growth from 
2025. The shared direction suggests a broad European listed-company trend, 
even though differences in score levels and timing persist between countries.

Figure 4: AI use intensity (0-10) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a geographical cross-check, our index matches the broad trends of the 
Eurostat ICT annual survey, which places Germany as a clear frontrunner among 
its large peers. But some differences do exist. For instance, Eurostat shows the 
Netherlands with a very high AI adoption. As Figure 5 demonstrates, there is a 
positive correlation between the AI use score metric derived here, and that from 
Eurostat, even though the Eurostat data measures if firms are adopting AI, and 
our new use measure also incorporates the concept of how intensively firms are 
using AI.

AI productivity impact: Reported benefits are rising, but 
transformational gains remain uncommon 
Higher intensity of use does not necessarily mean firms are benefiting more from 
AI. But our AI productivity impact metric does show a similar pattern to our AI 
usage metric, although it tends to be slightly lower, indicating that companies 
may not yet be reaping the full rewards for their AI investment. 
Financials stand out more clearly on reported productivity benefits than on AI-use 
intensity, while German companies again record relatively strong scores, but only 
in more recent history.

Figure 6: AI's impact on productivity (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

AI productivity impact across European industries 
Reported AI-productivity benefits have become more prominent across 
industries. Figure 6 shows scores rising gradually during 2022–25, followed by 
11 November 2026

indications of a faster increase from late 2025. The profile resembles the AI-use 
trend in Figure 4, suggesting that the stronger adoption narrative is accompanied 
by more discussion of operational gains. 
The aggregate trajectories of the AI use and AI productivity impact scores are 
similar, but sector rankings differ. Technology’s early relative strength in AI use is 
not matched by its productivity score, while financials report above-average 
productivity benefits despite more modest use scores. 
AI productivity impact across countries 
Figure 7 plots the self-report impact of AI on company productivity by country. 
Between 2022-23, German companies recorded average productivity impact, 
even though their AI use score was relatively high. However more recently, their 
AI impact score has persistently been above average. The UK grouping shows 
persistent early outperformance in AI impact during 2022–23, followed by 
convergence towards the aggregate average.

Figure 7: AI's impact on productivity (0-10) by country

AI labour force impact: On net, AI may be benefiting 
employment, for now… 
Across industries and countries, the recorded score for AI’s impact on the labour 
force remains relatively small. On average, the reported impact on companies’ 
employment has been fairly neutral, with a marginal negative bias over the last 
year or so.

Figure 9: AI's impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 11

Deutsche Bank AG Page 11

AI labour force impact across European industries 
Despite rising productivity, AI has not triggered the negative employment shock 
feared by the public. Aggregate labour impact scores across nearly all sectors 
hover near zero, with a slightly positive balance over history, and a slightly 
negative balance more recently in 2025-26 (Figure 9). But overall the magnitude 
of AI’s impact on labour remains low. For the STOXX 600 aggregate average, the 
score’s maximum deviation from 0 (no impact) is 0.43 on our 0-5 scale. 
AI labour force impact across countries 
Workforce-impact scores are also small across the main country groupings, with 
most countries slowing a slight negative tilt in recent years (Figure 10).

Figure 10: AI impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

The lack of significant impact so far from AI on companies’ labour forces so far is 
consistent with results from our previous work looking at the other side of the 
labour market. Data from our dbDataInsights household survey showed that 
workers across Europe reported a fairly low level of fear about losing their job to 
AI over the next year. The analysis in that report found results that were more 
consistent with workers adapting to the new AI world by upskilling and 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 12

Deutsche Bank AG Page 12

demanding higher wages. Those results would not be consistent with a world in 
which AI was actively replacing labour. 
These findings of a low impact on labour are highly consistent with recent 
academic research (e.g., Yotzov et al., 2026), which found that nearly half of 
surveyed UK and German firms reported absolutely no impact on headcount from 
AI over the past three years, 
Conclusion 
The newly developed framework provides a recurring way to monitor company 
disclosures about AI’s impact on productivity and labour. The central finding of 
this report is a broadening AI transition rather than clear evidence of an 
economy-wide productivity breakthrough. The new metrics indicate that AI use 
remains limited but is rising across the European countries and industries 
represented in the sample. Companies report low but growing productivity gains 
and little aggregate workforce impact so far. This pattern is consistent with a 
technology J-curve, with firms still integrating the new technology, rather than 
proof that this trajectory will unfold. Limited reported workforce effects today do 
not rule out subsequent layoffs or wider organisational restructuring. 
Quarterly updates to this framework will track Europe’s AI transition and provide 
timely signals of whether companies are reporting more substantial productivity 
and employment effects. 
Beyond AI, the framework could be adapted to extract signals about employment 
decisions, investment decisions and the general economic outlook from earnings 
calls. These potential extensions could form the basis of alternative nowcasting 
models for important macroeconomic data; they are applications that will be 
explored in future work.

11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

WESTPAC ECONOMICS

2026: FINAL ESTIMATE
Headline growth holds up, but momentum is fading fast
20 November 2026
Key points
• Westpac-Now points to GDP growth
of around 0.5%qtr in Q3 2026 (with
a 70% confidence interval of 0.1%qtr
to 0.9%qtr), unchanged from the

previous estimate. Growth has held
up, but the underlying flow of data
points to a clear loss of momentum
as the quarter has progressed.
• The Monthly Activity Index has
deteriorated through 2026 and is now
at its weakest level since October
2013, outside the pandemic period.
Weakness has become increasingly
broad-based, with business conditions
and confidence among the largest drags.
• Labour market conditions have also
softened, with the unemployment

rate reaching 4.9%, but have held up
better than model dynamics would
imply. Put differently, the model
would typically have expected a
larger deterioration in labour market
conditions given the weakness evident
elsewhere in the economy. This could
indicate that the labour market is
responding to the economic slowdown
with a longer-than-usual lag meaning
further deterioration is still to come.
• Downside risks to growth are building.
The model-implied probability of a

negative quarter rises from around
16% in Q3 to 30% in Q4, compared
with historical averages of just 8%
and 7%, respectively. However, the
probability of a technical recession
remains relatively low at around 5–6%.
How these risks evolve will depend
critically on whether the recent loss of
momentum persists through year-end.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
In November 2025, we introduced Westpac-Now (see here),
a continuously-updated measure of current activity in the
Australian economy. Westpac-Now draws on more than
60 high-frequency economic and financial variables, using
advanced statistical techniques to provide a timely pulse

check on economic momentum. It is the first Australian
model to incorporate internal banking data, enhancing its
predictive power.
This note provides the second estimate of GDP growth for the
September quarter 2026, using domestic and international
data released up to and including the October Labour Force
Survey (released 19 November 2026). Updates are published
monthly (see Appendix A for the release schedule).
Westpac Monthly Activity Index: momentum has

turned
After showing signs of improvement through the second
half of 2025, our Monthly Activity Index has deteriorated
progressively over 2026, with particularly sharp step-downs
in January, April and August. There were some signs of
stabilisation around mid-year, but these proved short-lived,
with the Index falling to –0.8 in September and –1.0 in
October. Outside the pandemic period, this is the weakest
reading since October 2013.
Importantly, the weakness has broadened through the

September quarter and into Q4. Softer labour-market indicators
have been accompanied by weaker business conditions,
confidence and household spending, suggesting the loss of
momentum has become increasingly broad-based. This is
particularly evident in our heatmap, where negative contributions
Momentum fades as downside risks build
have become both more widespread and more pronounced
through 2026, intensifying in August and September.
Our indicator heatmap decomposes movements in the Monthly

Activity Index into group-level contributions, helping identify
which parts of the economy are driving changes in activity. For
ease of interpretation, individual indicators are grouped into
broad economic categories (see Appendix for further details).
For example, the labour market category combines official
ABS Labour Force Survey outcomes with timelier survey-
based measures of labour demand and job advertisements.
Weakness broadens, but surprises are more mixed

The heatmap tells us where conditions are weak, while our
“surprise” analysis tells us where the data have been better or
worse than expected. For each indicator, our model compares
the realised outcome with its forecast and calculates the
surprise. The “Kalman gain” then determines how much
weight the model should place on that new information, taking
account of how informative or noisy the indicator typically is.
On this basis, business activity and confidence have surprised

materially to the downside, while labour-market, spending and
trade indicators have generally been stronger than expected.
In other words, an indicator can remain weak in absolute terms
while still delivering a positive surprise relative to the model’s
expectations.
What does this mean? This could indicate that the labour
market is responding to the broader slowdown with a longer-
than-usual lag, with this week’s weakness potentially a
downpayment on further deterioration ahead.

Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Westpac-Now: growth holds up, but momentum is
fading
Recall that Westpac-Now is a refined indicator that combines
our Monthly Activity Index with advanced econometric
techniques to estimate GDP growth. As previously noted,
the fit is not expected to be exact. Measured GDP includes

components that are not well captured by high-frequency
activity indicators, such as imputed rents for owner-occupiers,
housing-related ownership transfer costs, public and business
inventories, and depreciation.
To bridge monthly and quarterly data, we use a MIDAS
framework, allowing us to combine mixed-frequency inputs
and incorporate partial information for the current quarter
when producing GDP forecasts.
Based on current model estimates, Westpac-Now’s second

estimate for Q3 2026 points to GDP growth of 0.5%qtr (with a
70% confidence interval of 0.17%qtr to 0.94%qtr) and 2.1%yr,
in line with the second estimate and the 0.4%qtr (2.1%yr)
outcome recorded in Q2 2026. While aggregate growth
appears to have held up through Q3, the underlying picture
became softer as the quarter progressed.
With several headwinds now bearing on the economy
simultaneously, there is a growing risk that the slowdown in
activity becomes more pronounced than currently anticipated.

At the same time, the strength of the business investment
upswing provides an important counterweight that should
help cushion the broader slowdown.
Looking ahead, model dynamics suggest growth will tick
down to 0.3%qtr in Q4 2026 before picking up in Q1 2027.
However, these projections remain much more tentative,
given their reliance on projected inputs rather than realised
data. In addition, heightened uncertainty, particularly around
the renewed tensions in the Middle East conflict, means risks

around these forward estimates are larger than usual.
Downside risks are building
The sharp loss of momentum has materially increased downside
risks to growth. The probability of a negative quarter rises
from around 16% in Q3 to 30% in Q4, compared with historical
model-implied averages of just 8% and 7%, respectively. At
around the 95th percentile historically, the Q4 result signals an
unusually high risk of contraction over the quarter.
However, the probability of two consecutive negative quarters

remains much lower, at around 5–6%, leaving a technical
recession far from the central case. But the risks are building.
Around 30% of October indicators are still to be released,
and if they confirm or deepen the loss of momentum evident
in the Monthly Activity Index, the economy could move closer
to outright contraction. Alternatively, upside surprises in the
remaining data could reduce the likelihood of contraction. Note
that probabilities incorporate the model’s historical forecast

errors at the equivalent forecast horizon, capturing uncertainty
around each quarterly nowcast.
Q3 data: momentum faded as the quarter progressed
Data released in the first month of the September quarter
indicated that the economy has picked up momentum relative
to the previous quarter, with our first nowcast estimating Q3
growth at 0.6%qtr, in line with the 0.6%qtr “pure forecast”
based solely on data available up to June 2026.
However, the flow of data since then shows downside risks to

growth have begun to materialise. Incorporating the August,
followed by the September, data both yielded nowcasts

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
of 0.5%qtr. This contrasts with Q2 2026, when weakness
emerged early in the quarter, in April, before subsequent data
pointed to stabilisation.
Note that monthly activity estimates are more uncertain than
normal when inflation is shifting. Faster price growth can cause

nominal indicators, such as consumer spending, to rise more
strongly even though ‘real’, inflation-adjusted spending may
be softening (and vice versa). The inclusion of ‘real’ indicators,
such as employment and banking transaction volumes, helps
mitigate some – but not all – of this uncertainty.
Bottom line:
Westpac-Now points to GDP growth of around 0.5%qtr
(2.1%yr) in Q3 2026, suggesting growth held up reasonably
well. Beneath the surface, however, momentum deteriorated

sharply as the quarter progressed, with weakness broadening
across the economy. Business investment remains an
important counterweight, but the accumulation of headwinds
points to softer growth ahead. Westpac-Now currently points
to growth of around 0.3%qtr in Q4, with downside risks now
unusually elevated. Our central case remains one of slower
growth rather than outright recession, but the risks of a more
material downturn are building.

Appendix A: Westpac-Now Release Schedule (2026)
Going forward, Westpac Now will be published on the Friday following each ABS Labour Force Survey (LFS) release, at 11:30am AEDT/AEST (Canberra time).
Each release incorporates the latest labour market data alongside other high frequency indicators and provides an updated nowcast for quarterly GDP growth.

As the quarter progresses, each update should be interpreted as a progressively more complete estimate of that quarter’s economic performance.

## Document 531: 住宅価格も金利も上昇、住宅ローン減税はどこまで効くか ～控除率引き上げという選択肢を考える～

Source PDF: all report pdf/住宅価格も金利も上昇、住宅ローン減税はどこまで効くか ～控除率引き上げという選択肢を考える～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年11 月 10 日

1/7 
住宅価格も金利も上昇、住宅ローン減税はどこまで効くか 
～控除率引き上げという選択肢を考える～ 
 資産運用調査部 フェロー  谷口 智明

（要旨） 
○ 住宅価格の高騰と住宅ローン金利の上昇が重なり、住宅取得の負担が増している。本稿では、
住宅ローン減税の控除率と借入限度額（控除対象となる年末ローン残高の上限額）に着目し、
現行制度の負担軽減効果を確認した上で、今後の住宅取得支援の在り方を考察する。 
○ 住宅ローン減税は、借入限度額までの年末ローン残高に控除率を掛けた額を、所得税等から
差し引く仕組みだ。試算では、借入限度額を 4,500 万円とし、ローン残高は年間を通じて一
定、計算上の控除額は所得税等から全額差し引けると仮定する。控除率 0.7％、借入額4,000
万円では、金利が 0.5％から 1.2％へ上昇すると、利息に占める減税額の割合は 140％から約
58％へ低下する。金利 1.2％で借入額が 6,000 万円に増えると、借入限度額の制約もあり約
44％となる。控除率を 1.0％へ引き上げれば、それぞれ約83％、約63％へ高まる。なお、納
税額の制約で控除を使い切れない世帯では、負担軽減効果は小さくなる。 
○ 住宅ローン減税の控除率は、2022 年度税制改正で 1％から 0.7％に引き下げられた。これは、
低金利下で控除額が支払利息を上回る「逆ざや」への懸念等を背景とする。0.7％は当時の借
入金利等を踏まえて設定された水準であり、金利環境が変化した現在、逆ざやの抑制と住宅
取得支援のバランスを再検討することには合理性がある。 
○ 国土交通省の調査では、住宅ローン減税の利用者等の 21％が、制度がなければ住宅を購入し
なかったと回答した。購入判断等への影響が示唆される一方、2024 年度の税収減は約 1 兆円
に上る。また、住宅投資額の押上効果に関する既存の検証成果は制度の意義を考える重要な
蓄積だが、環境変化に応じて検証を継続し、制度の評価に生かす必要がある。 
○ 住宅価格と金利の同時上昇への対応策として、例えば、既存住宅の取得者も含む新規入居者
を対象に、取得当初の一定期間、控除率を 0.7％から 1.0％へ引き上げることが考えられる。
借入限度額の引き上げでは減税額が増えない家計にも、支援を厚くする手段となる。また、
納税額の制約で減税額が増えない世帯への対応として、取得時の補助等による支援も検討課
題といえる。住宅取得への効果と財政負担を継続的に見極めながら、住宅価格や金利環境の
変化に即して支援の対象や水準を見直し、制度の実効性を高めていくことが求められよう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1．住宅購入に重なる「価格高騰」と「金利上昇」 
住宅を購入しようとする家計は、住宅価格の高騰と住宅ローン金利の上昇という二つの変化に
直面している。国土交通省の不動産価格指数（2010 年平均＝100）によれば、2025 年12 月の全国
の住宅総合は 148.0、マンション（区分所有）は 225.1 となった（資料 1）。

資料 1 不動産価格指数（全国・住宅）の推移 
（注）季節調整値。2025 年 10～12 月は速報値。 
（出所）国土交通省「不動産価格指数」をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

地域や住宅の種類によって差はあるものの、とりわけマンション価格の上昇が際立つ。例え
ば、後述する住宅ローン減税（住宅借入金等特別控除）の控除率が 0.7％に引き下げられた 2022
年の初めから 2025 年末までに、マンション価格は約 3 割上昇している。 
住宅購入に必要な資金も大きい。国土交通省「令和 7 年度住宅市場動向調査」によれば、購入
資金の平均は土地取得を伴う注文住宅で7,646 万円、分譲集合住宅（分譲マンション等）で6,443
万円に上り、住宅取得に多額の資金を要することがうかがえる（注 1）。 
これに金利上昇が重なる。日本銀行は 2024 年3 月にマイナス金利政策を解除し、その後の段階
的な利上げを経て、2026 年 9 月には政策金利を1.25％程度まで引き上げた。新規借入向けの変動
金利は、大手銀行の公表例では 1％台に達している。住宅価格上昇で必要な借入額が膨らめば、金
利上昇による負担も一段と重くなる。 
こうした環境の下で、住宅ローン減税は住宅取得を目指す家計の負担をどこまで和らげるの
か。本稿では、控除率と借入限度額（控除対象となる年末ローン残高の上限額）に着目し、現行
制度の負担軽減効果を確認する。その上で、制度見直しの経緯や住宅取得への影響を踏まえ、今
後の支援の在り方を考察する。

2．住宅ローン減税の負担軽減効果をどう捉えるか 
住宅ローン減税は、一定の要件の下で年末ローン残高の 0.7％を所得税等から税額控除する仕組
80

### Page 3

3/7 
みである。2026 年度税制改正では適用対象となる入居期限が 2030 年まで延長された。例えば、
2026 年入居で新築の長期優良住宅・低炭素住宅を取得した一般世帯の場合、借入限度額は 4,500
万円、控除期間は 13 年間である。子育て世帯や若者夫婦世帯には借入限度額の上乗せがあり、前
述の場合、借入限度額は 5,000 万円となる（注 2）。 
控除率と借入限度額は、金利や借入額の変化に対する負担軽減効果を左右する。借入限度額は
控除を受ける個人ごとに適用され、住宅の区分等によって異なる。ここでは、1 人で住宅ローンを
借り入れて新築の長期優良住宅・低炭素住宅を取得し、子育て世帯等への上乗せを適用しない場
合を想定する。金利・借入額別の単年の負担軽減効果について、控除率0.7％と 1.0％の場合で比
較した（資料 2）。

資料 2 借入額・金利・控除率別にみた利息と住宅ローン減税額（単年の機械的試算） 
（注）借入初年で、ローン残高は年間を通じて一定と仮定した機械的試算。年間利息＝ローン残高×金利、最大減税額＝min（ロ 
ーン残高、4,500 万円）×控除率（0.7％または 1.0％）。2026 年に新築の認定住宅へ入居し、1 人で借り入れる場合を想定。
子育て世帯・若者夫婦世帯に係る借入限度額の上乗せは適用せず、税額控除を満額利用できると仮定。ペアローン等によ
り複数人が控除を受ける場合、世帯全体の減税額は異なる。元金返済、手数料等は考慮していない。「利息－減税額」は住
宅取得に伴う総費用や実際の返済額を示すものではない。 
（出所）国土交通省「令和 8 年度税制改正要望結果等」をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

控除率0.7％、借入額 4,000 万円の場合、金利が0.5％から 1.2％へ上昇すると、年間利息は 20
万円から48 万円へ増える。最大減税額は 28 万円で変わらず、年間利息に占める減税額の割合は
140％から約58％へ低下する。同じ金利1.2％で借入額が6,000 万円に増えると、年間利息は 72
万円となる。借入限度額 4,500 万円を超える部分には控除が及ばず、最大減税額は 31.5 万円にと
どまるため、年間利息に占める減税額の割合は約 44％へ低下する。 
控除率を1.0％へ引き上げれば、同じ借入額・金利でも減税額が増えることから、年間利息に占
める割合は高まる。 
なお、納税額の制約で控除額を使い切れない場合、負担軽減効果はさらに小さくなる。

3．なぜ控除率は 1％から 0.7％に引き下げられたのか 
住宅ローン減税の控除率は 2022 年度税制改正（原則として 2022 年入居分から適用）で1％から
0.7％に引き下げられた。背景の一つは、資料2 でみたように、超低金利下で控除額が支払利息を
上回る「逆ざや」である。会計検査院は、2017 年に控除の適用を開始した調査対象 1,748 人の

4/7 
78.1％が金利1％未満で借りていたことを指摘し、不要な借入や繰上返済の先送りを促すおそれを
問題視した（注3）。 
2022 年2 月 15 日の衆議院財務金融委員会で、政府は控除率を 0.7％とした理由について、金利
0.7％以上の適用者が全体の「6 割強」を占めていたことを挙げた。また、借入に伴う事務手数料
や保証料も加味し、不要な借入を促しにくい水準として 0.7％を設定したと説明した。さらに、実
際の支払利息を控除上限とする方法は制度が複雑になるため採用しなかったとも答弁している
（注 4）。つまり0.7％は、当時の借入金利や事務手数料・保証料等の借入費用と、制度の簡素性
を踏まえた水準であったといえる。 
それでは、近年の借入金利はどのように変化したのだろうか（資料 3）。

資料 3 借入金利の分布 
（注）インターネット調査。2026 年 1 月調査は、2025 年 4～9 月に住宅ローンを借りた全国の 20 歳以上 70 歳未満の人（学生・無
職を除く、1,237 人）が対象。「年 1.5％超」は、元資料の 4 区分を合算。端数処理により合計が 100％にならない場合がある。 
（出所）住宅金融支援機構「住宅ローン利用者調査（2026 年 1 月調査）」をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

控除率が引き下げられた時期に近い 2022 年 10 月調査と 2026 年 1 月調査を比べると、「年 0.5％
以下」は減少する一方、「年 0.5％超～1.0％以下」および「年1.0％超～1.5％以下」は大きく増
加している。ただし、「年 1.5％超」は減少していることに加え、金利区分は 0.7％を跨ぐため、
金利 0.7％以上の利用者の割合は把握できない。なお、2026 年 1 月調査は 2025 年4～9 月の借入
を対象としており、その後の金利上昇は反映されていないため、足元では、より高い金利で借り
入れた人の割合が増えていると推察される。また、既存の変動金利利用者も、適用金利の見直し
を通じて、利息負担が増えている可能性がある。 
住宅価格と金利が上昇するなか、控除率0.7％の設定時とは環境が変化している。控除率の水準
については、借入金利の分布や事務手数料・保証料等を含む借入費用の実態、税収減などを踏ま
え、改めて検討する意義があろう。

### Page 5

5/7 
4．減税は住宅取得を後押ししているか～約 1 兆円の税収減との関係 
国土交通省「住宅税制の活用状況等に関する意識調査（2025 年）」 では、2022～2025 年の住宅
取得・入居者（予定者を含む）のうち、住宅ローン減税の利用者および利用予定者に、住宅ロー
ン減税がなかった場合の影響について尋ねたところ、「購入しなかった」が 21％、「住宅購入や新
生活に影響が生じた」が 59％、「影響はなかった」が 20％だった。「影響が生じた」と回答した人
には、購入価格や住宅性能を下げる、家具・家電、生活費・教育費等を減らすといった回答がみ
られた。こうした結果は、住宅ローン減税が、購入判断だけでなく、住宅の選択や購入後の暮ら
しにも影響を与えている可能性を示している。 
住宅ローン減税には相応の財政負担も伴う。国土交通省（2025）によれば、2024 年度の税収減
（見込額を含む）は国税 8,280 億円、地方税1,790 億円の計 1 兆 70 億円に上る。同制度がない場
合に比べた住宅投資額の押上効果は、分析方法や仮定の異なる複数の試算で約 0.9～5.8 兆円と推
計されている。同省は、データの制約等から他の支援措置の影響を切り分けることには限界があ
るものの、こうした推計やアンケート結果を踏まえ、住宅取得の促進や内需拡大等に一定の効果
があると評価している。 
これらの検証成果は、制度の意義を考える上で重要な蓄積である。ただし、住宅投資額の増加
は、そのまま税収増を意味するわけではない。財政負担との関係を捉える際には、各試算の前提
や対象とする効果の範囲に留意し、検証成果を制度の評価に生かす必要がある。

5．住宅価格と金利の同時上昇にどう応えるか 
住宅価格と金利の同時上昇により、住宅取得の負担が増している。住宅ローン減税の控除率
0.7％は低金利下の逆ざやを抑える観点等から見直されたが、住宅価格や金利環境が変化した現
在、逆ざやの抑制と住宅取得支援のバランスについて、改めて検討する余地がある。 
例えば、負担増への対応策として、控除率の水準や適用期間は慎重に見極める必要があるもの
の、既存住宅の取得者も含む新規入居者を対象に、取得当初の一定期間、控除率を0.7％から
1.0％へ引き上げることなどが考えられる。資料 2 の試算が示すように、控除率の上乗せは、借入
限度額の引き上げだけでは減税額が増えない家計への支援を厚くする手段となる。返済残高が大
きい取得当初の負担を軽減することで、住宅取得を目指す家計の購入判断を後押しする効果も期
待できる。 
また、納税額の制約で控除率を上乗せしても減税額が増えない世帯への対応として、取得時の
補助等による支援も検討課題といえる。その際、所得等を踏まえた対象の設定や事務負担といっ
た制度設計上の諸課題を整理する必要があろう。 
住宅取得支援は供給増につながることもあるが、OECD（2022）は、供給を増やしにくい地域で
は支援の一部が住宅価格に転嫁され、購入者の負担軽減効果が弱まる可能性も指摘している（注
5）。地域の供給状況を踏まえ、既存住宅の流通・改修の促進等と組み合わせ、家計が取得可能な
住宅の選択肢を広げることも重要だ。 
住宅ローン減税とその効果検証を一体として捉えることも欠かせない。住宅取得への効果と財

### Page 6

6/7 
政負担を継続的に見極めながら、住宅価格や金利環境の変化に即して支援の対象や水準を見直
し、制度の実効性を高めていくことが求められよう。

## Document 546: 八个月配额后，刚果金出口全面暂停，钴市再迎政策变数

Source PDF: all report pdf/八个月配额后，刚果金出口全面暂停，钴市再迎政策变数.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
刚果⾦宣布 11 ⽉ 30 ⽇起再度暂停所有钴出⼝，期限暂未确定。此前实施的配额制度未能阻
⽌钴价下跌，钴氢氧化物价格⾃ 4 ⽉⾼点已近乎腰斩。全球需求疲软、库存增加及再⽣钴供
应增⻓持续施压，印尼或将受益于供应格局变化。
摘要：
全球最⼤钴⽣产国刚果⺠主共和国宣布，将于本⽉底起再次暂停钴出⼝，以遏制国际钴价持续
下跌，并缓解全球市场供应过剩压⼒。
11 ⽉ 23 ⽇，据彭博报道，刚果政府监管机构 ARECOMS 周⼀发表声明称，⾃ 11 ⽉ 30 ⽇起暂停所
有钴出⼝，暂停期限暂未确定。ARECOMS 表⽰，此举旨在 “ 解决国际市场供应过剩问题，推动
市场可持续再平衡 ” 。这是刚果⾃ 2025 年 2 ⽉⾸次实施全⾯出⼝禁令以来，第⼆次采取类似措
施。
此次禁令出台之际，钴价正⾯临较⼤下⾏压⼒。刚果主要出⼝产品钴氢氧化物的价格⾃今年 4 ⽉

触及峰值后⼏近腰斩，全球需求疲软及再⽣钴供应增加进⼀步加剧了市场供需失衡。新⼀轮出
⼝管控将影响全球钴供应，也可能改变矿企和下游买家的库存及采购安排。
价格持续下跌，配额制度未能稳住市场
刚果于 2025 年 2 ⽉⾸次实施全⾯钴出⼝禁令，⼋个⽉后以严格配额制度取⽽代之，但未能阻⽌钴
价持续回落。根据 Fastmarkets 数据，钴氢氧化物价格在⾸次禁令实施后⼀度上涨逾四倍，随后
从 4 ⽉⾼点⼤幅回落，⼏乎抹去此前涨幅。
ARECOMS 在声明中表⽰，此次决定是综合考虑钴价⾃ 6 ⽉以来显著且持续下跌后作出的。当前
市场⾯临全球钴消费放缓、库存增加及投机活动等多重压⼒。该机构已于 11 ⽉ 9 ⽇召开会议，批
准此次临时出⼝暂停决定。
新⼀轮禁令也将波及主要钴⽣产商。⽬前持有最⼤出⼝配额的企业包括洛阳钼业、嘉能可及欧
亚资源集团。现⾏配额制度已将刚果钴出⼝量限制在 2024 年全国产量的⼀半以下。
刚果政府数据显⽰，今年前九个⽉，该国矿企共出⼝钴 61441 吨，远低于 2024 年同期的逾

145,000 吨。此次再次暂停出⼝，意味着全球钴原料供应将进⼀步受到限制。
需求疲软，印尼或填补部分供应缺⼝
钴主要⽤于电动汽⻋及消费电⼦产品的可充电电池。分析师指出，消费电⼦领域需求尤其疲
软，是近期钴价承压的重要原因之⼀。
供应端⽅⾯，刚果此前约占全球矿产钴供应量的四分之三。作为全球第⼆⼤钴⽣产国，印度尼
西亚或将从新⼀轮禁令中受益。该国依托本⼟镍矿资源，在冶炼过程中⽣产含钴中间产品，有
望填补刚果出⼝暂停留下的部分供应缺⼝。
ARECOMS 表⽰，计划在四个⽉后重新评估禁令，届时将综合考量钴市场⾛势、库存⽔平及全
球供需平衡状况。同时，该机构还将利⽤出⼝暂停期审查并调整配额分配机制。

## Document 549: 内外経済ウォッチ『日本～春闘は「上げ方」も焦点に～』(2026年12月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/内外経済ウォッチ『日本～春闘は「上げ方」も焦点に～』(2026年12月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

日本　 
～春闘は「上げ方」も焦点に～
　連合は2027年春闘（春季生活闘争）において、全体の
要求目安を「6％中心・5％以上」とした。5％以上の要求は
これで4年連続だ。労働側の要求と賃上げ実績は、近年概
ねパラレルに動いている。根強い物価上昇と深刻な人手
不足を背景に、来春も高めの賃上げが続く公算が大きく
なっている。
　もっとも、春闘の焦点は「上げ幅」だけにとどまらなく
なっている。連合は2027年、率で示す「上げ幅の指標」に
加え、年齢別の到達賃金で示す「水準の指標」を一段強化
した。初めて2030年までの中期の賃金水準目標を掲げ、
所定内賃金を現行水準から15％程度引き上げ、中位数
で35歳36万円・30歳34万円への到達をめざす。年齢ご
との到達額を示すことで、水準の低い層を引き上げ、格差
を是正しようという発想だ。企業側でもベースアップや初
任給の大幅引き上げ、若年層への重点配分、非正規の時
給改善など、限られた原資を「どの層に厚く配分するか」
が人材戦略に直結する経営課題となっている。
来年の春闘も高い賃上げ率へ
　こうした「上げ方」の違いは、家計の実感にも表れてい
る。勤労者世帯の収入階層別に実質可処分所得の変化を
みると（資料2）、2019年から2025年にかけて、最も低い
層（第1分位、＋3.7％）と最も高い層（第10分位、＋2.5％）
はプラスを確保した一方、第2～第9分位の中間層は総じ
てマイナスとなった。低所得層は最低賃金の大幅な引き
上げ、高所得層は共働き増による配偶者収入の増加など
が効いた。はざまの中間層は、賃上げが物価上昇に追い
つききらず、生活実感が相対的に弱い。連合総研の調査
でも、賃上げが物価高に追いついていないと感じる人の
割合は50代・60代前半で7割弱に達し、20代を大きく上
回る。連合が中期目標で「分厚い中間層の復活」を掲げた
背景もここにある。
　一昔前のような「一律ベア」へ全面的に戻ることは、人
材獲得競争が激しさを増すなかで考えにくい。ただ、メリ
ハリの効いた賃上げは、中間層の底上げという課題と背
中合わせでもある。「上げ幅」に加えて「上げ方」も賃上げ
動向を見るうえで重要になってきている。
「U字型」の実質可処分所得
経済調査部　主席エコノミスト　星野 卓也（ほしの たくや）
資料1 春闘賃上げ率と要求水準（2027は目安）
（出所）連合より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成。

資料2 年収階層別・実質可処分所得の伸び
（出所）総務省より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成。
5 第一ライフ研レポート 2026.12
内外経済ウォッチ
内外経済ウォッチ

## Document 566: 凱基投顧_9933 中鼎_賴偉中_20261116

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_9933 中鼎_賴偉中_20261116.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 16, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R      Powered by EFA Platform 1

動態更新 工程服務‧台灣

中鼎 
(9933.TW/9933 TT)

毛利率結構性提升，獲利大幅上修 
重要訊息 
中鼎於 11/16 召開法說會。 
評論及分析 
毛利率維持高檔且有高額業外利益，3Q26 財報優於預期。中鼎 3Q26 毛利
率 13.4%、QoQ-0.3ppts，維持今年以來站穩 13%以上的表現，營業利益
季增 7.7%至 24.73 億元，優於凱基預期及市場共識 20.1%、4.1%。業外
有銅衍生商品避險，認列公允價值評價利益 4.3 億元， EPS 2.17 元、
QoQ+32.3%、YoY+173.2%，優於凱基預估及市場共識 49.5%、43.6%。 
高毛利率並非一時，獲利上修。中鼎累計前三季毛利率 13.39%，大幅高於
過去十年平均值之 6.98%，且單季均超過 13%。管理層說明毛利率提升並
非一次性因素，而是 2024 年起接案毛利率提升，且對原物料充分避險，隨
著高毛利專案陸續進入工程而帶動毛利率結構性優化。基於毛利率假設上
修、3Q26 營收獲利優於預期、洽談中專案金額季增超過 20%，我們將中鼎
2026-27 年 EPS 上調至 6.64 元、7.09 元，上修幅度 24.6%、21.7%。 
能源政策轉向具共識，中鼎為國內少數具核電建廠實績與認證廠商。賴總統
上任以來核能政策逐漸轉向，並提到如有共識可重啟核電。我們認為一旦
11/28 非核家園公投通過，民意支持下，能源政策必將轉向擁核。後續包括
核三重啟、核四評估改建、引入 SMR(（Small Modular Reactor ）等都將成
為選項，預期將衍生數千億元龐大工程需求。中鼎為國內少數具發電廠及核
電廠建廠實績公司，有望深度參與能源轉向核能的商機。 
投資建議 
毛利率結構性提升具持續性，我們上修中鼎未來兩年獲利預估。且隨著大型
政府標案陸續進入工程期，預期 2027 年營收將恢復年增，營運動能顯著轉
強。我們將中鼎投資評等由「持有」上調至「增加持股」，目標價由 43 元
上調至 65 元。評價基於 2.1 倍 2027 年底每股淨值，位於十年歷史本淨比區
間中上緣，相當於 9.2 倍 2027 年本益比，或 7.1% 2026 年凱基預估配發現
金股息殖利率。 
投資風險 
應收帳款逾期、GEH 倒帳、三元電池廠訴訟案。 
增加持股‧調升 
  收盤價 November 16 (NT$)  51.50 
12 個月目標價 (NT$) 65.00 
前次目標價 (NT$) 43.00

上漲空間 (%) 26.2

焦點內容  
1. 中鼎 3Q26 毛利率維持高檔，且業外
有衍生性商品避險收益 4.3 億元，EPS
達 2.17 元，大幅優於預期。 
2. 基於毛利率結構性提升，我們將
2026-27 年 EPS 大幅上修至 6.64 元、
7.09 元。 
3. 能源政策轉向具共識，中鼎為國內少
數具核電建廠實績與認證廠商。

圖 4: 公司概況  圖 5: 2025年中鼎在建工程存量大幅提升 
中鼎成立於 1979 年，為台灣最大的統包工程公司，提供客戶
工程規劃、設計、設備採購、施工、試車、操作、保養一貫化
的完整服務(EPC)。工程類型聚焦於煉油廠、石化廠、發電廠、
焚化爐。除工程外，集團透過轉投資子公司崑鼎(6803 TT，
NT$267，未評等)及新鼎(5209 TT，NT$167.5，未評等)等，跨
足綠能環保業務及系統整合業務。目前中鼎最新股本為 96.18
億元。

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

### Page 1

十一月 17, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

法說重點摘要

大聯大 
(3702 TT, NT$81.2, 增加持股)

焦點內容 
1. 大聯大 3Q26 營收季增 11.8%，超出財測上緣並優於市場預期，主
要受惠記憶體漲價及 AI 需求成長。毛利率季持平達 4.42%，獲利
季增 20.5%至 103.9 億元，EPS 為 5.96 元。展望 4Q26，公司給
出營收財測中位數將季減 0.9%，毛利率 4.35-4.55%，並預期排
除一次性利益後，可持續性獲利仍將增加，EPS 為 5.13-5.81 元。 
2. 營收結構方面，隨漲價與供給吃緊延續，記憶體營收占比已提升至
58%，算力(含伺服器)占比亦提升至 52%。管理層表示記憶體比重
提升係反映全球半導體結構變化，若未來記憶體價格反轉，對公司
庫存跌價損失有限，然而毛利率可能將微幅下滑。 
3. 展望中長期發展，大聯大將聚焦全球化、客戶延伸及供應鏈服務。
公司認為目前僅服務一家 CSP 客戶，市佔率低因此長期成長空間巨
大。LaaS 方面，1-3Q26 GMV 年增 75.6%至 US$42.6bn，滲透率
約 3.4%，未來隨觸及更多原物料供應業務，將可拓展公司可服務
市場。 
結論 
大聯大積極發展供應鏈服務，並持續擴大海外布局及 CSP 客戶延伸。
儘管目前升息環境可能造成財務成本增加，然而 AI 需求仍將支撐公司
發展。目前股價交易於市場共識 2027 年每股盈餘 18.6 元的 4.4 倍本
益比。

3Q26 財報 
 營收：5,100 億元，季增 11.8%，年增 108.6% 
 毛利率：4.42%，與 2Q26 持平，年增 0.39ppts 
 營業費用：80.7 億元，季減 2.1%，年增 79.1% 
 隨營收與獲利成長，獎金費用增加 
 營業利益率：2.84%，季增 0.22ppts，年增 0.65ppts 
 業外損失：16.8 億元 
 財務成本 40.3 億元，占營收比重約 0.79% 
 投資收益 24.4 億元，主要來自交換債轉換的一次性利益 
 獲利：103.9 億元，季增 20.5%，年增 226.9% 
 EPS：5.96 元，Non-TIFRS EPS( 扣除 3Q26 交換債一次性項目)為
5.29 元 
 對比 2Q26 為 5.12 元，3Q25 為 1.89 元 
 ROWC：17.6%，ROE：43.4% 
 營運週轉天數：56 天，對應 3Q25 為 68 天

Q&A 
 未來的成長動能？ 
 公司將聚焦全球化、代理以外的供應鏈服務，以及客戶延伸(尤
其為雲端客戶) 
 公司認為目前在整體供應鏈市場的占比仍小，因此成長空間巨大 
 股利政策？ 
 公司希望股利發放隨 EPS 提升而增加，確切金額將待董事會公告 
 LaaS 營運狀況？ 
 目前僅揭露營收與 GMV 
 公司認為整體供應鏈需求強勁，未來將積極投入並隨供應鏈拓展
至海外 
 可轉債的資金用途？ 
 將盡快投入國內外轉投資事業，並以投入效益為優先 
 公司記憶體占比較高，若市場反轉的影響為何？ 
 記憶體占比提升係反映全球半導體結構變化 
 記憶體業務採 ship & debit 模式，因此無庫存跌價損失疑慮，惟
毛利率可能微幅下滑 
 公司債買回及庫藏股計畫？ 
 資金將優先用於提升獲利能力及主要成長業務，以提升 EPS 與股
利 
 反映於員工持股信託自市場買進金額逐年增加 
 升息對財務費用影響？ 
 財務成本占營收比重隨營收與獲利成長達 0.8%(近期低點為
0.65%，過往最低為 0.25%，升息高峰約為 1%)

徐子捷 
jay.hsu@ctbcsis.com

系統代工 
廣達 (2382 TT)

重新覆蓋 
買進(Buy) 
AI機櫃放量與 AI代理驅動 2027年的成長 
⚫ 3Q26營收創新高，但高單價機櫃稀釋毛利率 
⚫ AI/通用伺服器雙成長帶動 2027年伺服器營收佔比衝高 
⚫ 非 GPU解決方案預估 2027年將成長至雙位數佔比 
3Q26 營收創新高，但毛利率受筆電成本上漲與高單價機櫃而降低：
廣達 3Q26營收 1.1155兆元(QoQ+7.6%，YoY+125.2%)，營收創歷史
新高。毛利率 4.75%(QoQ-0.27ppts，YoY-2.10ppts)，毛利率走低主要
因為筆電材料成本上揚、出貨規模下滑，以及高單價的 AI伺服器比
重拉高而稀釋毛利率。營業利益率為 2.89% (QoQ-0.41ppts，
YoY-0.82ppts)。稅後歸屬母公司淨利為 284.2 億元(QoQ-0.8%，
YoY+73.0%)，淨利率 2.55%(QoQ-0.21ppts， YoY-0.77ppts)；受發行
GDR稀釋股數影響，EPS由 2Q26的 7.43元降至 7.08元。 
AI/通用伺服器呈強勁成長且占比將繼續提升，但筆電消費市場疲弱：
前九月伺服器佔整體營收已過 80%，其中 AI伺服器佔整體伺服器比
重介於 75%~80%。3Q26筆電出貨 960萬台(QoQ-17%，YoY-24%)，
主因 2H26消費市場需求疲弱及記憶體等材料成本上揚。4Q26的 AI
伺服器受惠新產品單價提升，營收維持強勁成長，但處新舊產品轉換
期，總出貨櫃數將較 3Q26略減，毛利率預期略下滑，營業利益率則
持平或略低於 3Q26。預估 2027年 AI/通用型伺服器皆雙位數年增，
主要受惠新世代 GPU伺服器機櫃陸續放量加上 AI代理帶動通用型伺
服器需求同步強勁，伺服器總營收佔比將攀升至 85%~90%，其中 AI
伺服器佔比可望達 80%以上。筆電出貨預估呈雙位數季減，2027年筆
電出貨預計年減高個位數至 10%內且對總營收貢獻將降至個位數百分
比。  
非 GPU解決方案將強勁成長：2026年非 GPU (ASIC)佔 AI伺服器營
收仍小於 10%(接近高個位數)，2027年將成長至雙位數佔比，且非
NVIDIA解決方案明年將有雙位數的強勁成長。AMD方面今年已有出
貨，明年亦會持續出貨，各家 CSP皆會採用作為多元解決方案之一。
我們考量 2027年整體伺服器成長動能強，雖然筆電市場疲弱但因占
營收比持續下降而影響有限，故給予買進評等，目標價為 375元(2027
年 EPS32.64元 x11.5倍 PER)。風險因子：伺服器需求放緩。

2 
 公司背景 
廣達電腦成立於 1988年，總部位於台灣桃園，並於 1999 年在台灣證
券交易所掛牌上市。早期以筆記型電腦設計與代工起家，後續逐步拓
展至雲端運算、企業級伺服器及 AI運算系統，現已由傳統筆電代工廠
轉型為 AI伺服器與雲端基礎設施供應商。旗下設有全資子公司雲達科
技 (QCT)，負責資料中心設備、混合雲軟硬體整合及 5G企業專網基礎
設施業務。截至 3Q26營收組成包含伺服器 (約占 80%)、筆記型電腦 (約
占 10%~15%)、車用電子及其他新興應用 (約 5%~10%)。

廣達採台灣研發、全球後段組裝的產能布局:台灣桃園總部 (華亞廠 )定
位為全球新產品導入中心，聚焦於高階 AI伺服器研發、SMT主板生產
與 L6/L10半成品組裝，目前產能維持滿載，並負責 GB300等次世代產
品的前期試產。美國田納西廠與加州廠則專責北美 CSP 客戶的伺服器
後段組裝(L10/L11)與系統測試 (Rating)，因應北美算力建置需求，已於
2026 年完成產能擴建，並進一步增加液冷散熱測試機台與整櫃測試產
線。歐洲德國廠目前聚焦於車用電子組裝與歐洲主權 AI 資料中心的
伺服器後段組裝 (L11)，其車用電子產線已進入量產期，伺服器產線則
配合歐洲本土混合雲需求進行彈性調配。泰國廠定位為分散地緣政治
風險、承接非中國製造訂單的海外主要基地。該廠主力負責伺服器主
機板(Motherboard)的 SMT表面黏著製程，透過持續擴增的主板產能，
就近且穩定地供貨給美歐市場的後段系統組裝廠(L10/L11)。中國上海
廠與重慶廠主要做傳統筆記型電腦的前段製造、組裝及消費性電子產
品，目前產能利用率受 PC市場影響表現平穩，營運焦點轉向導入自動
化製程以降低人工成本。

廣達撤銷友達華亞廠收購案：廣達原定以 197億元收購友達華亞廠 (預
計 4Q27點交、2028年投產)，但雙方對規格與交期無共識，最後決議
撤銷友達華亞廠收購案，轉向海外據點加大投資，2028 年全球產能規
劃不變。2026年底 AI產能較 2025年底倍增目標可如期達成；3Q26資
本支出 128億元，前九月累計 346億元，美國加州與泰國廠佔總支出
約 80%。預估 2027年資本支出介於 400億 ~450億元(相較 2026年的 400 
億元僅微幅增加 0%~12.5%)，主要投入電力升級與高階冷卻測試設施
以因應新世代機櫃的測試時間增長、耗電量大增。

北美 CSP 資本支出未縮減，伺服器需求依然強勁 : 北美四大雲端服務
供應商持續擴建資料中心。 2027 年北美四大 CSP 整體資本支預估
YoY+37%。目前未見調整明年訂單且主要 CSP 財報亦未縮減資本支
出，顯示需求依然強勁。因建廠與電力審批需 18~36 個月，所以廣達
採接單生產(Build-to-Order)模式，客戶還提供 12~24個月的產能預留承
諾，雙方互信與約束力高。伺服器業務占廣達總營收超過 80%，其中
AI伺服器佔伺服器營收約 75%~80%。下一代 VR200平台預計於 4Q26
年小量出貨，並且在 1Q27放量。整體而言，廣達伺服器營收占比逐漸
提高，預估 2027年伺服器總營收佔比將攀升至 85%~90%，其中 AI伺
服器佔比可望達 80%以上。

圖表一 :四大 CSP CAPEX預估(單位:USBn)

資料來源:中信投顧彙整  
AI 代理與新產品的 ASP 大增帶動伺服器營收成長：通用型伺服器
2H26 的轉強主要受惠市場對 AI代理的需求上升，帶動資料處理與
一般運算伺服器的配套需求。預計 2027年通用型伺服器在出貨量及
營收上皆有雙位數成長；目前 x86與 CSP自研 ARM CPU架構的需
求同步增加。新世代機櫃 (如 VR200相較 GB300)的 ASP幾乎翻倍，
在 Buy and Sell模式下，高單價關鍵零組件放大分母，毛利率被稀
釋，但營收與營業利益金額將顯著成長。為緩解高單價對營運資金
與毛利率的壓力，公司正積極與客戶洽談將最昂貴的零組件 (如 GPU
及記憶體)轉為寄售模式，目前已有部分專案談成，預期 2027 年寄
售比例將進一步提升，但現階段仍以 Buy and Sell為主。  
筆記型電腦業務受制於成本上漲與提前拉貨 : NB業務占廣達總營收比
重約 10%~15%。品牌客戶在 1H26 預期記憶體價格上漲而提前拉貨，
使 2Q26出貨達 1,150萬台高基期，2H26轉趨保守。3Q26筆電出貨 960
萬台(QoQ-17%，YoY-24%)，反映消費市場需求疲弱及記憶體等材料成
本上揚。記憶體(DRAM、 NAND)與 CPU供應持續受 AI伺服器排擠而
價格上漲，且消費性 PC成本轉嫁難度較高。預估 2026年全年筆電出
貨量將呈現雙位數年減， 2027年則年減高個位數至 10%以內。

圖表二、廣達筆電出貨量預估 (單位:千台)

## Document 691: 木头姐“倒在黎明前”：卖出英伟达后黄仁勋回归

Source PDF: all report pdf/木头姐“倒在黎明前”：卖出英伟达后黄仁勋回归.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

此前，⽊头姐旗下 ARK Invest 卖出约 8500 万美元英伟达股票，并转投 SpaceX 。⽽⻩仁勋
回归后，英伟达股价单⽇⼤涨超 30% 。
摘要：
⼀场围绕英伟达领导层的⻛波，意外演变成 ARK Invest ⼀次颇具戏剧性的投资失误。
11 ⽉ 23 ⽇，英伟达董事会全票通过恢复⻩仁勋 CEO 职务，推翻此前不打算让其复职的表态。消
息公布后，英伟达股价单⽇涨超 30% 。⽽就在约⼀周前， Cathie Wood （⽊头姐）旗下 ARK
Invest 刚以担⼼ “ 失去⻩仁勋 ” 为由卖出约 8500 万美元英伟达股票，转⽽买⼊ SpaceX 。
Wood 随后承认，促成这笔交易的⻛险 “ 已经消失 ” 。她此前担忧，⻩仁勋可能加⼊⻢斯克团队，
不仅带⾛英伟达的核⼼⼯程师，还可能导致 SpaceX 将业务转向其他供应商。但这⼀情景并未发
⽣，⻢斯克本⼈甚⾄曾签署请愿书，要求恢复⻩仁勋的职位。
与此同时，英伟达基本⾯依然强劲，第三财季营收同⽐翻倍，管理层进⼀步上调⻓期增⻓预
期。不过， CEO ⻛波平息并不意味着所有问题都已解决，监管调查、财务披露和公司治理⻛险

仍悬⽽未决。
ARK 押注⻩仁勋离职，交易逻辑迅速逆转
ARK 此次减持的核⼼逻辑，是担⼼⻩仁勋离职后，英伟达将失去⼈才和重要客⼾。据报道援引
Wood 在 Money Rehab 播客中的表态，她表⽰，卖出英伟达完全是因为担⼼失去⻩仁勋，以及其
他公司借机夺⾛英伟达的市场份额。
她特别提到⻢斯克，称后者曾表⽰愿意邀请⻩仁勋加⼊团队，可能协助其 TeraFab 项⽬。Wood
还解释， ARK 此前增持英伟达，部分原因是 SpaceX 承诺其轨道数据中⼼独家使⽤英伟达芯⽚。
她担忧，⼀旦⻩仁勋加⼊⻢斯克阵营， SpaceX 也可能将相关业务转向其他供应商。
11 ⽉ 16 ⽇， ARK 卖出约 8500 万美元英伟达股票，同⽇旗下旗舰创新基⾦买⼊约 4680 万美元
SpaceX 股票。⻩仁勋复职后， Wood 承认此前促成交易的⻛险已经消失。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

值得注意的是， SpaceX 仍是英伟达客⼾， Vera Rubin 系列产品正在 SpaceXSI 陆续部署。此次
复职决定则是在美国政府⾼层提供意⻅反馈后作出，董事会同时肯定了⻩仁勋的沟通⻛格与领
导能⼒。
英伟达业绩强劲， SpaceX 增⻓亮眼但估值更⾼
英伟达第三财季营收达到 1158.4 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 103.2% ；⾮ GAAP 每股收益为 2.68 美元，⾼
于市场⼀致预期的 2.47 美元。公司预计第四财季营收达到 1300 亿美元。
管理层还将 2028 财年营收增⻓预期从约 70% 上调⾄约 75% 。这意味着，即使经历领导层⻛波，
英伟达仍在持续上调对未来业务增⻓的判断。
ARK 转投的 SpaceX 同样表现强劲。公司第三季度营收达到 146 亿美元，⾼于市场预期的 130.2 亿
美元； GAAP 净利润为 44 亿美元，⽽去年同期为亏损 11 亿美元， AI 业务营收同⽐增⻓ 830% ⾄ 76
亿美元。
但增⻓之外，估值差异也值得关注。 SpaceX 市值约 1.79 万亿美元，英伟达约 4.01 万亿美元；英

伟达单季净利润已达 652.87 亿美元，过去 12 个⽉市盈率约 25 倍，⽽ ARK 买⼊时 SpaceX 的远期市
盈率约 48 倍。
⻩仁勋回归之外，监管与治理⻛险仍待厘清
CEO 职位争议虽已平息，但围绕⻩仁勋的调查仍未结束。英伟达董事会表⽰，独⽴委员会将继
续调查相关事项，直⾄最终结案。
据路透社报道，美国证券交易委员会（ SEC ）正在调查英伟达潜在的会计问题；据 TIKR ，公司
的季度报告提交也出现延迟。此外，英伟达披露的⻛险清单显⽰，公司⾯临约 3900 亿美元的供
应承诺敞⼝，以及最⾼ 1180 亿美元的担保⻛险。
公司治理层⾯也存在不同声⾳。 Chamath Palihapitiya 在 All-In 播客中批评董事会的复职决定，认
为不能如此轻率地将⼀名⾼管重新安排到⼀家估值如此庞⼤的上市公司，这相当于抛弃治理规
则，让所有⼈承担⻛险。
ARK 此次操作也显⽰，基于单⼀⼈物去留做出投资决策存在较⼤不确定性。 Wood 此前担忧的⻩
仁勋离职、加⼊⻢斯克团队并带⾛核⼼⼯程师等情景均未发⽣。接下来，英伟达财报延迟提交

的原因、独⽴调查结果及第四财季营收能否达到 1300 亿美元指引，仍值得关注。

### Page 3

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 716: 科技股重挫亚太股市，韩股下挫5%，台股指跌8%，三星电子大跌9%，软银重挫16%

Source PDF: all report pdf/科技股重挫亚太股市，韩股下挫5%，台股指跌8%，三星电子大跌9%，软银重挫16%.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

MSCI 亚太指数下跌 2% ，抹去周⼆创下 7 ⽉以来最⼤单⽇涨幅的全部涨势。⽇股半导体封装
基板⻰头揖斐电电⼦单⽇暴跌 17% ，存储芯⽚巨头铠侠重挫 15% 。⽇本第⼀⼤半导体设备
巨头东京电⼦⼤跌  13.09% ，硅晶圆⼤⼚胜⾼亦下挫 12.23% 。 WTI 原油跌超 1.4% ，报 80.8
美元。
摘要：
亚太股市周三⼤幅下跌，此前美国科技股遭遇重创，市场对⼈⼯智能投资能否带来⾜够回报的
疑虑再度升温。与此同时，投资者还需消化潜在贸易消息影响。
周三，韩国综合股价指数⼤跌 5.4% ，领跌亚太市场。⽇本⽇经 225 指数跌逾 3% 。 MSCI 亚太指数
下 跌 2% ， 抹 去 周 ⼆ 创 下 7 ⽉ 以 来 最 ⼤ 单 ⽇ 涨 幅 的 全 部 涨 势 。 中 国 台 湾 地 区 股 指 下 跌 8% ⾄
36,892.58 点。
此轮抛售延续了华尔街的跌势，隔夜纳斯达克综合指数下滑 1.5% ，英伟达单⽇跌幅近 6% ，费城
半导体指数重挫 7.9% ，⾃ 6 ⽉⾼点以来累计跌幅已达约 40% 。

市场⽬光现已转向周三美国将公布的个⼈消费⽀出（ PCE ）价格指数，以及潜在的关税措施，

幅拉升的半导体设备、存储芯⽚及数据中⼼光通信赛道快速撤离。

避险情绪推升美债，⻩⾦⼩幅反弹

交易时段⾛势。
（市场）每天都令⼈眼花缭乱。从周⼀到现在，基本⾯并没有发⽣根本性变化，但我们还未⾛出
困境。围绕 AI 交易的恐慌与不确定性仍需消化。
资本⽀出的担忧、数据中⼼的建设、资本成本与投资回报率之间的背离，以及市场内部盈利与亏
损板块之间的分化 —— 这才是更宏观、更核⼼的故事。

持续的⽅式维系。

115 年前 9 月手機、筆電及平板電腦進口值年增 23%、6%，平均單價上漲為要因 
1. 隨科技快速發展，消費性電子產品在功能、規格及應用上持續演進，國內市場需求亦隨各類產品
更迭與消費者偏好而變化。手機兼具通訊、影音娛樂及數位服務等功能，為我國頗具規模之進口
消費品，其金額由 109 年 28.7 億美元增加至 114 年 34.4 億美元，近 4 年數量大約 6 百萬支左右；
今(115)年 1-9 月進口值 25.4 億美元、年增 2 成 3，數量(385 萬支)大致持平，顯示進口值成長幾乎
來自平均單價上漲，除與新機上市、高階機種占比提高等因素有關，亦反映 AI 需求排擠效應推升
之記憶體等零組件成本壓力。筆電及平板電腦受新冠疫情帶動遠距商機，進口值於 110 年明顯成
長，111 年達 21.1 億美元高點，今年前 9 月進口值年增 5.9%，平均單價隨成本而調升應是主因。

## Document 807: 阴霾笼罩年底市场！摩根大通：伊朗军事升级、美联储紧缩、政府关门、关税上调、芯片管制一起来了！

Source PDF: all report pdf/阴霾笼罩年底市场！摩根大通：伊朗军事升级、美联储紧缩、政府关门、关税上调、芯片管制一起来了！.pdf

### Page 1

摩根⼤通警告， 12 ⽉ 9 ⽇⾄ 11 ⽇前后是伊朗军事升级的潜在窗⼝，油价可能短暂冲⾼后回
落；美联储预计再加息三次⾄ 4.50%-4.75% ，但持续紧缩可能重演 2018 年末政策失误；
2027 年 AI 资本开⽀将近 1.4 万亿美元；关税有效税率年底前可能升⾄ 15%-20% ，芯⽚管制
范围正向远程算⼒和 AI 基础设施延伸；美国政府关⻔⻛险因伊朗战争拨款争议上升。上述
⻛险叠加可能造就 “ ⼀个⾮常难看的圣诞节 ” 。
摘要：
距离 2026 年结束还有五周，市场却要同时⾯对⼀张密密⿇⿇的⻛险清单。
据追⻛交易台消息， 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇，摩根⼤通全球研究主席 Joyce Chang 率团队在华盛顿与政策制
定者、官⽅债权⼈及独⽴智库⼈⼠举⾏了⼀整天的闭⻔会议。该⾏于 11 ⽉ 24 ⽇发布报告，将会
议核⼼内容整理为 " 中期选举后需监测的⼗⼤⻛险 " 。
报告开篇基调沉重。分析师写道， " 从华盛顿回来后，我们对中期选举后的⻛险更加谨慎 " ，⽽那
⼀周⼏乎每⼀类资产都在下跌，美债和美元是仅有的例外。

报告援引当天最令⼈印象深刻的⼀句警告："AI 融资的解体，加上美联储的进⼀步紧缩，可能造
就 ' ⼀个⾮常难看的圣诞节 '—— 股市⼤幅下跌，直到美联储被迫转向，就像 2018 年末那样。 "
伊朗： 12 ⽉可能是最危险的窗⼝
与会者为伊朗局势勾勒出三条路径：谈判达成和解（概率 20%-25% ）、维持现状（ 30%-40% ）、
美国重启军事⾏动（ 30%-40% ）。
谈判前景之所以渺茫，在于双⽅诉求差距悬殊 —— 美国坚持 " 经济绞杀 " 策略，伊朗则要求⽴即解
除封锁、撤销制裁、美军撤出地区。报告描述伊朗领导层 " 越来越被专注于政权存续的伊斯兰⾰
命卫队强硬派所主导 " 。
时间节点上，与会者特别点出了12 ⽉ 9 ⽇⾄ 11 ⽇。理由直⽩：这⼏天⽉相处于当⽉最暗的夜
晚， " 历史上有利于进攻⽅ " 。若特朗普总统授权⾏动，与会者认为更可能是针对伊朗剩余核设施
的 5 ⾄ 6 天短暂密集空袭，⽽⾮涉及地⾯部队的持久战 ——" 美军伤亡的可能性让地⾯部队的部署
在政治上极不吸引⼈ " 。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

油价⽅⾯，该⾏⼤宗商品策略师 Natasha Kaneva 的团队泼了⼀盆冷⽔。他们预测 2027 年和 2028
年全球⽯油将分别出现 160 万桶 / ⽇和 140 万桶 / ⽇的供应过剩，布伦特原油年底 2027 将跌⾄66 美
元 / 桶。报告指出， " 当前油价已将攻击概率计⼊其中，考虑到 9 ⽉油市已转为供应过剩、 10 ⽉过
剩进⼀步扩⼤，现货价格⾼出合理⽔平 5 ⾄ 7 美元 " 。
短期军事升级可能带来油价的暂时性冲⾼，但基本⾯的供应过剩压⼒将随后主导⾛势。
美联储：加息加到 “ 出错 ” 的⻛险
美联储的路径争议是本次华盛顿会议的另⼀焦点。
主张美联储落后于曲线的⼀⽅认为，由于美国财政缺乏约束，中性利率可能⽐疫情前⾼出 100 ⾄
150 个基点，政策传导也因企业和家庭可绕过政策利率获得其他融资⽽减弱。⼀位与会者预期美
联储将再加息三次，每次 25 个基点，分别在 12 ⽉、 1 ⽉和 3 ⽉⾄ 4 ⽉。摩根⼤通美国经济团队的基
准预测与此⼀致：三次加息后，联邦基⾦利率⽬标区间升⾄4.50%-4.75%，随后暂停⾄年底。

但另⼀派的担忧同样尖锐。伊朗冲突将油价从约 60 美元 / 桶推升⾄ 100 美元 / 桶以上，触发了美联
储措辞的明显转变，但 " 冲击主要集中在整体通胀，核⼼通胀相对稳定 " 。
这派⼈⼠指出，若油价回落⾄ 60 美元 / 桶，将通过能源价格和基数效应⼤幅压低整体通胀；若跌
⾄ 40 ⾄ 50 美元 / 桶，则可能通过运输和其他能源敏感成本带动更⼴泛的通缩， " 核⼼通胀有可能在
其他分项保持稳定的情况下接近 2%" 。
" ⻛险在于，美联储根据滞后数据持续加息，⽽供给冲击恰恰在此时逆转。 "
报告还提到，⾸次购房者进⼊市场的年龄已推迟⾄ 40 多岁， 7.5% 左右的抵押贷款利率和结构性
供应不⾜是主要障碍。在能源冲击已是暂时性的背景下，⽤加息来打压通胀，代价由住房市场
承担，这⼀逻辑受到质疑。
AI 融资： 7 万亿美元的⼤厦，地基有多稳？
摩根⼤通信贷策略团队预测，2027 年⾄ 2031 年间 AI 资本开⽀总额将达 7 万亿美元，仅 2027 年就
约 1.4 万亿美元，⼏乎是 2026 年的两倍。

问题是，钱从哪⾥来。报告指出， " 每年 1.4 万亿美元的融资规模，已经触及债务市场所能承受的
上限 " ， AI 投资尚未实现⾃我造⾎， 2027 年和 2028 年的融资组合中将有更⼤⽐例来⾃股权资本。
⽬前信贷⽀持安排存量约3720 亿美元。
更令⼈警惕的是循环融资结构。报告援引 IMF 的研究： IMF 审查了 2023 年以来的 14 笔相关交易，
发现约 3000 亿美元的股权投资和 6500 亿美元的债务，换取了 1.4 万亿美元的未来收⼊承
诺。 "IMF 警告，杠杆和这些融资关联可能在 AI 投资或盈利不及预期时放⼤市场调整。 "
供应商融资、⽆追索权贷款和租赁安排引发了另⼀层担忧 —— 这些安排让企业以⾼价购⼊资
产，同时将⼤部分下⾏⻛险转移给贷款⽅， " 形成融资⽀撑⾼交易价格、⾼价格反过来验证更⾼
估值的⾃我强化循环 " 。
Mag 7 公司在 2026 年第三季度财报期的盈利增速，⾃ 2022 年以来⾸次低于标普 500 其余成分
股。

### Page 3

关税：年底前有效税率可能升⾄ 15%-20%
关税不是在被逆转，⽽是在被重建。
该⾏经济学家 Jahangir Aziz 和 Abiel Reinhart 在 11 ⽉ 13 ⽇的报告中指出，预计⼀系列 301 条款和
232 条款关税即将宣布，以将平均有效关税税率恢复⾄ IEEPA 被法院推翻前的 2 ⽉⽔平。
具体⽽⾔，当局正在对 16 个经济体的 " 结构性产能过剩 " 展开 301 条款调查，结果即将公布；与此
同时， 232 条款调查范围也在扩⼤，涵盖钢铁、铝、铜等产品，有效关税税率可能从当前 7%-8%
升⾄ 15%-20%。
加拿⼤⽅⾯可能获得⼀定豁免，⺠主党也可能寻求废除对加拿⼤的 338 条款关税作为早期政治胜
利，但整体⽅向是关税上⾏，⽽⾮逆转。
芯⽚管制：在新国会就职前抢先出⼿
英伟达争议在两党之间形成了加强出⼝管制的政治动⼒，⽩宫有动⼒在 1 ⽉ 3 ⽇新国会就职前率
先划定规则。
管制范围正在从传统芯⽚出⼝扩展⾄远程算⼒访问、芯⽚制造设备和 AI 基础设施投资。报告提

到四项相关法案可能被纳⼊《国防授权法案》（ NDAA ），包括覆盖美国数据中⼼远程访问的《远
程访问安全法》（ RASA ）、针对 AI 技术管控的《 AI 监察》法案、针对先进半导体制造设备的
MATCH 法案，以及打击芯⽚⾛私的相关⽴法。
政府关⻔：市场低估了⻛险
12 ⽉ 11 ⽇是政府资⾦截⽌⽇期，也是国会与⽩宫的第⼀个正⾯冲突点。
新的⺠主党多数将以此作为筹码，核⼼争议在于《国防授权法案》（ NDAA ）。⺠主党⾯临两难：
既要为五⻆⼤楼拨款，⼜不想被视为授权伊朗战争继续进⾏。
报告指出，国会预算办公室（ CBO ）估计截⾄ 2026 年 8 ⽉ 1 ⽇，伊朗冲突已令国防部花费约380
亿美元，此后每⽉还将产⽣ 30 亿美元或更多⽀出；五⻆⼤楼⾃⼰的估计截⾄ 11 ⽉ 13 ⽇为483 亿
美元。若将对经济和消费者的更⼴泛影响纳⼊，宾⼣法尼亚⼤学沃顿预算模型的独⽴估算⾼达
2100 亿美元。

### Page 4

政府关⻔⻛险因伊朗战争拨款争议⽽上升，报告明确指出， " 关⻔⻛险对市场的冲击可能超出市
场当前的预期 " 。
财政：两党都不愿动⼑
美国财政⾚字 2026 年预计占 GDP 的6.2%，摩根⼤通经济学家预测 2027 年为 6.1% 、 2028 年升⾄
6.4% 。
与会者的⼀致结论令⼈沮丧：两党都没有政治意愿去削减⽀出以稳定⾚字。共和党难以削减社
保、医保和医疗补助；⺠主党的优先事项是恢复 ACA 补贴、逆转医疗补助和 SNAP 削减，这只会
增加开⽀。
财政部⻓⻉森特寄望于 "3-3-3" 策略 —— 额外增加 300 万桶 / ⽇能源产量、实现 3% 经济增⻓、将财
政⾚字压缩⾄ GDP 的 3%—— 但报告直⾔， "3% ⾚字⽬标在本届总统任期结束前远不可能实现 " 。
CBO 测算，若利率在其 2026 年 2 ⽉基准之上持续⾼出 50 个基点， 2026 年⾄ 2036 年间将累计增加
约1.6 万亿美元净利息⽀出、 1.9 万亿美元总⾚字；若⾼出 150 个基点，则分别增加约4.9 万亿美元

和6 万亿美元。
与会者的共识是：可能需要⼀次重⼤的市场或经济冲击，才能迫使政策制定者采取⾏动。
市场含义： 25% 的跌幅能承受吗？
摩根⼤通报告最后⽤了相当篇幅讨论市场的承受能⼒。
与会者普遍认为，若 AI 修正引发市场抛售，其性质更接近 2001 年⽽⾮ 2008 年 —— 不会触发系统
性危机，因为芯⽚等计算资产经济寿命约五年，估值扭曲的持续时间有限，银⾏资本充⾜，家
庭和企业资产负债表也⽐全球⾦融危机前健康。
但也有与会者提出更悲观的情景：美国股市市值已达 GDP 的250%（截⾄ 2026 年 6 ⽉），若 AI 融
资解体、科技出⼝管制收紧与美联储持续加息三者叠加，可能产⽣类似 2018 年末的更显著调
整。
哈佛经济学家 Jason Furman 的测算显⽰，美股下跌 25% 可能使消费在随后⼀年减少约 0.8% ，拖
累 GDP 增速约 0.6 个百分点；若考虑财富效应对消费的完整影响，冲击可能⾼达 3.4 个百分点。
他的假设情景还显⽰，若英伟达、微软、⾕歌、亚⻢逊、 Meta 、博通和甲⻣⽂这七⼤ AI 股下跌

50% ，可能带动⼤盘下跌约11%。
IMF 的模型则显⽰， AI 冲击可能触发美股下跌 20% 、其他市场下跌 15% ，美国 GDP 在 2028 年减
少 0.8% ，全球 GDP 减少 0.4% 。

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
